## Hill County, Texas — Locality Guide (1870–1906)

**Jurisdiction overview**
- **Formed:** 1853 from Navarro County
- **County seat:** Hillsboro
- **Parent state:** Texas
- **Population:** Not obtainable (population stats API unavailable); Texas was a rapidly growing post-Civil War state
- **Economy:** Cotton farming; Hill County was in the Blackland Prairie, a prime cotton belt
- **Key history:** Organized 1853; Hill County residents experienced post-Civil War Reconstruction, followed by the cotton boom of the 1880s-1890s. Files Valley is a small community in the county.

**Boundary changes:** Hill County's boundaries were stable after 1853 formation from Navarro County. Pre-1853 Hill County land was part of Navarro County — no overlap for this research window.

---

### Vital Records

**Marriages — EXCELLENT coverage**
FamilySearch holds fully indexed (100% recordSearchablePercent) and full-text searchable Hill County marriage volumes from 1873 through the 1930s:
- Vol. 1: 1873–1876 (IGN `004820309`) — Marriage indexes 1873-1941 also present
- Vols. 2–3: 1876–1886 (IGN `004820310`)
- Vols. 4–5: 1886–1892 (IGN `004820303`)
- Vols. 6–7: 1892–1897 (IGN `004820304`)
- Vols. 8–9: 1897–1902 (IGN `004820305`)
- Vols. 10–11: 1902–1908 (IGN `004820306`)
- Vols. 12–13: 1907–1912 (IGN `004820307`)
- Marriage licenses Aug 1895–Feb 1909 (IGN `004820616_004`) — 100%, full-text
- Also indexed in statewide: **Texas, County Marriage Records, 1837–2010** (FS coll. 1803985, 2.1M records) and **Texas, County Marriage Index, 1837–1977** (coll. 1803987)

**Deaths**
- Statewide: **Texas, Deaths, 1890–1977** (FS coll. 1983324) — 4.5M indexed records — covers Martha Wood's 1906 death
- Local Hill County deaths 1903–1938: IGN `004820614_001` and `004820614_002` — 100% indexed + full-text
- Additional local volumes 1903–1956 (IGNs `007611640_004/005`) — full-text but 0% name-indexed
- **Pre-1890 deaths**: No statewide registration; must rely on census mortality schedules, church records, or newspapers

**Births**
- Texas did **not** require statewide birth registration until 1903; no birth certificate exists for Jimmie Jewel Neal (b. 1884) unless a delayed certificate was filed
- Local Hill County birth records 1870–1940 (IGN `008992082`, 886 images) — full-text searchable, **not** name-indexed
- Hill County birth index 1903–1956 (IGN `008502334`) — full-text, not indexed
- Delayed birth indexes 1869–1972 (IGN `008413381`, 5,357 images) and multiple delayed birth volumes (IGNs `008152748`, `008507941–943`) — full-text searchable; **search these for any child born in Hill County before 1903**
- Statewide **Texas, Birth Certificates, 1903–1936** (FS coll. 1803956) covers births after registration began

---

### Census Records

| Year | Coverage | Access level |
|------|----------|-------------|
| 1860 | Hill, Texas | Browse-only images (0% indexed); 2 volumes, IGNs `004297439_004`, `004297452_017` |
| 1870 | Hill, Texas | **Indexed + images** (96%); IGN `004267882_003` |
| 1880 | Hill, Texas | **Indexed + images** (statewide FamilySearch) |
| 1890 | Hill County included in surviving multi-county TX fragment | Partial index (67%); IGN `004679013` |
| 1900 | Hill, Texas | **Indexed + images** (96–98%); IGNs `004118499_001`, `004118498_003` |
| 1910 | Hill, Texas | **Indexed + images** (statewide FamilySearch) |

---

### Tax Records (Census Substitutes)

- Hill County tax rolls 1853–1878 (IGN `004616581`, 1,685 images) — full-text, not name-indexed
- Hill County tax rolls 1883–1887 (IGN `004616582`, 1,538 images) — full-text, not name-indexed
- Hill County tax rolls 1891 (IGN `004616583`, 1,248 images) — full-text, not name-indexed
- Statewide **Texas, County Tax Rolls, 1837–1910** (FS coll. 1827575) — 4.5M **indexed** records; use this first before browsing the above volumes
- Tax rolls are especially valuable as 1890 census substitute and to track property owners annually

---

### Probate and Court Records

- **Texas, Probate Records, 1800–1990** (FS coll. 2016287) — 1.16M images, **browse-only**, not indexed
- Hill County civil court cases 1872–1912 (multiple IGNs: `008689333`–`008689341`) — full-text searchable, not name-indexed
- Probate can name heirs/relatives — worth browsing if other evidence exhausted

---

### Land / Deed Records

- Extensive Hill County deed records 1881–1913 (dozens of volumes) — **full-text searchable** but **0% name-indexed**; must use full-text search
- **Texas, County Tax Rolls, 1837–1910** (statewide, indexed) is the practical substitute for grantee/grantor deed indexes online

---

### Church Records

- Parish records for **Blum, Hill County** (Baptist) 1886–1902 (IGN `008984030`) — browse-only images
- **New Mexico and Texas, Select United Methodist Church Records, 1870–1970** (FS coll. 2534480/external Ancestry coll. 8974) — limited coverage
- Texas church records generally sparse on FamilySearch; local congregational archives may hold more

---

### Military Records

- **Texas, Civil War Service Records of Confederate Soldiers, 1861–1865** (FS coll. 1932381) — 956K indexed
- Confederate pension records: Texas State Library and Archives (indexed index available online)
- Hill County biographies 1892 in a multi-volume set (IGNs `007548348_001–006`) — browse-only, useful for prominent families

---

### Cemetery Records

- **Find a Grave** and **BillionGraves** both cover Texas cemeteries including Hill County
- Texas Gravestone Photo Project (texasgravestones.org) covers the state
- Files Valley Cemetery (where Martha J. Wood Hamby is reportedly buried) should be checked on Find a Grave

---

### Newspapers

- **Portal to Texas History** (texashistory.unt.edu) — digitized Texas newspapers, free
- **GenealogyBank** and **Newspapers.com** have Texas holdings with search capability
- Hill County had local papers in Hillsboro; obituaries and marriage notices are valuable

---

### Repository Guide (Online)

| Repository | Key collections | Access |
|---|---|---|
| FamilySearch | Marriage records 1873–1912 (fully indexed); Deaths 1890–1977 (indexed); Census 1870/1900 (indexed); Tax rolls 1837–1910 (indexed); Delayed births | Free |
| Ancestry | TX Marriage Index 1837–1965; TX Death Index; U.S. Federal censuses; TX Tax Rolls | Subscription |
| FindMyPast | TX marriages; TX tax rolls | Subscription |
| Find a Grave | Cemetery records for Hill County | Free |

### Physical Repositories

| Repository | Holdings | Access |
|---|---|---|
| Hill County Clerk, Hillsboro TX | Marriage licenses, deed records, court records | In-person / mail |
| Texas State Library and Archives Commission (Austin) | Statewide vital records, confederate pension files, county records on microfilm | In-person; some online |
| Texas DSHS | Birth and death certificates (post-1903 originals) | Online request |

---

### Known Record Losses / Gaps

- **No statewide birth registration before 1903** — largest gap for this research
- **1890 federal census mostly destroyed** (fire); Hill County is in the surviving fragment but only partially indexed
- **No death registration in Texas before 1890** statewide; local recording was county-dependent before that
- Hill County courthouse has not been reported to have burned; records appear largely intact

---

### Research Tips

1. **Marriage records are the strongest asset here**: Hill County marriages are 100% indexed from 1873; search both the statewide collection AND the local volumes for the 1879 Neal-Landham marriage and the 1869 Lanham-Wood marriage
2. **Martha J Wood had at least 3 marriages** (Wood→Lanham, Lanham→Neal, Neal→Hamby); search under all surnames: Wood, Lanham/Landham, Neal, Hamby
3. **No birth certificate for Jimmie (b. 1884)** — rely on census, death certificate (which names parents), and marriage records to document parentage
4. **Delayed birth records** (Hill County, IGNs `008152748`, `008413381`, `008507941–943`) may document births before 1903; search by full-text using names
5. **1900 census** is 96–98% indexed — essential for confirming Martha's household (she appears as Martha J. Hamby with husband William S. Hamby)
6. **Tax rolls** (statewide indexed, coll. 1827575) can track Wood/Lanham/Neal households annually in the 1870s–1900s
7. **Files Valley** is a small community in Hill County; for Martha's 1906 death, search both the local Hill County death volumes AND the statewide Texas Deaths 1890–1977

**assistant:** No existing plans; no prior log entries. Starting-point facts from tree: Martha J Wood (LKYG-VKB) linked as Jimmie's mother with multiple pre-loaded tree sources but zero GPS-extracted assertions. Goal: build a plan to confirm that parentage via original records.

Key locality facts from loc_001: Hill County marriage records 1873+ fully indexed (100%); Texas Deaths 1890-1977 indexed; 1870/1880/1900 census indexed; no birth certs before 1903; delayed birth records exist (full-text). Wiki API unavailable during locality survey.

Record-type guide for a parentage/maternity question: (1) death certificate of subject (names parents), (2) census household records showing child with parents, (3) parents' marriage record, (4) delayed birth records, (5) FAN/collateral.

Plan structure: 7 items, ordered by directness and availability.

**assistant:** Key findings from reads:
- **Death cert (FLBC-TZW)**: Only Jim Neal appears as father in the indexed persona — mother not captured in the index. Birth year shows "1855" (likely a transcription error; should be ~1884). The image must be read to get the mother's name.
- **Marriage (QV14-6CN3)**: Mr James M Neal + Mrs Mattie Landham, 31 Jul 1879, Hill TX. "Mrs" prefix confirms Mattie was a widow (of Landham).
- **NUMIDENT 6K72-N91L**: Claude Neal Ray, born 23 Sep 1883, "Falls Valley*" TX — parents: Jim Neal + Martha Unknown. An apparent older sibling of Jimmie.
- **NUMIDENT 6K4H-R49Q**: Dorothy Helen Barnes — parents: Elmer B Yarbro + Jimmie J Neal. One of Jimmie's daughters; confirms Jimmie's identity.
- **NUMIDENT 6K4N-5DRN**: Willie Mae Yarbro — parents: Elmer B Yarbro + Jimmie J Yarbro. Another daughter.

Logging all five records, then delegating extractions.
